[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F51.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just not sleeping right anymore. I work overnights, so I’m used to my sleep being kind of backwards, but this is different. I’ll get off a shift feeling like I should be exhausted, like my body is heavy and I need to crash, and then I lie down and I’m just wide awake. I’ll be staring at the ceiling, turning over and over, and it feels like my mind won’t shut off even though I’m so tired.\n\nAnd then when I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I keep waking up scared, like startled awake, and I don’t even feel rested after. It’s like I’m sleeping in little broken pieces, and then I wake up feeling like I never slept at all. It’s been wearing me down, and I didn’t really know what else to do, so… I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced anything particularly stressful or traumatic recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not one big thing that I can point to and say, “That’s when it started,” if that’s what you mean. Nothing like an accident or somebody attacking me or anything like that.\n\nI’ve been trying to think about it because people keep asking, like, “Did something happen?” and I feel bad because I don’t have a good answer. My life has stress, sure, but it’s more just regular stuff piled up, I guess. Work can be a lot, especially doing overnights and taking care of people when you’re already tired, but I wouldn’t call it one specific traumatic thing recently. It’s more like… I don’t know, everything just started feeling heavier, and then the sleep got worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring nightmares or disturbing dreams?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly, because I don’t always remember a whole dream like a movie or anything. It’s more like I’ll finally drift off after lying there forever, and then I wake up scared, like my body jolts awake before my brain even catches up. Sometimes I’m not even sure what I was dreaming about, I just wake up with that awful feeling, like something bad was happening or about to happen.\n\nIt’s been happening enough that sleep doesn’t feel like sleep anymore. I get off an overnight shift and I should be exhausted, and I am exhausted, but I’ll still lie there wide awake for so long. Then when I do sleep, it’s all broken up, and I wake up scared, and by the time I’m supposed to get up, I feel like I never rested at all. So… maybe they’re disturbing dreams, or maybe it’s just waking up scared, but either way it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge when you're awake, outside of the times when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t think of myself as anxious all day, if that makes sense. I get stressed like anybody, especially with work and trying to function when I’m tired, but I don’t know that I’m walking around feeling panicky or anything.\n\nIt’s more noticeable when I’m trying to sleep, or when I start thinking about whether I’m going to be able to sleep. During the day, or when I’m awake and doing things, I guess I can feel tense sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being worn down. I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. So… maybe a little on edge at times, but I wouldn’t know how to separate it from being exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving or re-experiencing a moment from your past as if it were happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people describe it, like suddenly being back there and seeing it all again. I don’t really have a moment like that that comes back. At least not that I can name.\n\nSometimes I get stuck thinking about things, like going over stuff in my head or feeling bad about things that happened, but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually there again. It’s more just thoughts, or feelings, or me trying to figure out why I feel the way I do. I don’t get, like, pictures taking over or anything where I lose track of where I am.\n\nI guess if you mean do I have memories that bother me, maybe sometimes, but not like reliving something. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right. I just don’t think that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things, places, or activities because they make you feel uncomfortable or remind you of something unpleasant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in a clear way. I mean, I avoid things sometimes, but I think it’s more because I’m tired or I don’t feel up to dealing with people, not because a place reminds me of something specific.\n\nLike, if I don’t go somewhere, it’s usually because it feels like too much effort, or I’m worried I won’t have the energy to get through it, or I just don’t want to be around noise and conversation. But I can’t think of a certain place or activity where I’m like, “I can’t go there because it brings something back.” It’s not like that.\n\nThere are things that make me uncomfortable, sure, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed myself avoiding a particular street, or a certain kind of room, or people because of some memory. If I’m avoiding anything, it’s more just… life, I guess. But not because it reminds me of something unpleasant, at least not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're in a safe environment?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not constantly. I mean, I notice things around me sometimes, like if someone is acting weird or if I’m walking somewhere and it feels uncomfortable, but I don’t think I’m always scanning for danger, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMost of the time when I’m in a safe place, I don’t feel like something bad is about to happen from outside, exactly. I might feel uneasy or tense sometimes, but I can’t say I’m watching doors or checking every sound or looking for threats all the time. It’s more like I’m in my head, or I’m just tired and trying to get through whatever I’m doing.\n\nI guess I do pay attention if I’m alone somewhere or if it’s late, but that feels kind of normal to me. I wouldn’t say I’m on guard all the time in places I know are safe. At least I haven’t noticed that about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy doing or places you used to go that you no longer do or visit because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s actually been one of the bigger things, I think. I used to have more of a routine outside of just work and going home, and now it feels like everything has kind of narrowed down to just trying to make it through the day.\n\nI used to go to church more, and that used to feel like something that kept me connected to people. I wasn’t always super involved every single week, but I went, and I knew people there, and it felt like a place I could show up. Lately I just… I don’t go. It feels like too much to get myself together and be around everyone, and then I feel bad because I know people probably notice, or maybe they don’t, I don’t know. But I’ve pulled back from that.\n\nAnd Zumba too. I used to like that, even if I wasn’t great at it. It was something fun, something where I could move around and not think so much. But I haven’t been going. I tell myself I’m too tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t. It just feels impossible to add anything extra when I’m already barely handling work.\n\nI was mentoring too, and I’ve backed away from that. That one makes me feel really guilty, because it’s not just me missing a class or something. It’s like I was supposed to be there for somebody, and now I can barely be there for myself. I just get home and I’m done. I don’t have anything left in me.\n\nEven work has gotten hard in a way that scares me, because I’m a personal care aide, so I have to be present and careful with people. And lately I can’t keep up with my care visits the way I should. I don’t feel like I can do them safely anymore sometimes. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I got so overwhelmed and stuck that I couldn’t finish it. I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. And then after that I don’t feel rested at all, even if technically I slept some.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in, because it’s not just “I had a bad night of sleep.” It’s like my sleep doesn’t work right anymore. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. Like, I’ve heard people describe that as watching themselves from above or feeling like they’re not in their own body, and I can’t really say that’s happened to me.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of distant or not fully present, especially when I’m worn down, but I still feel like I’m me, inside my own body. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that outside-my-body feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s like my mind just keeps going back over every little thing I’ve ever done wrong, or could’ve done wrong. Especially with the patients I’ve taken care of. I’ll think about something small, like if I didn’t check on somebody fast enough, or if I sounded irritated when I shouldn’t have, or if I missed something I should’ve noticed. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I do it with my family too. I think about ways I should’ve helped more, or protected people better, or been more patient, or done something different. I keep replaying things and feeling like I failed them. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. It feels like God is mad at me. Like He sees all the ways I didn’t protect people right, or didn’t do enough, and He’s angry with me for it. I know people might say I’m being too hard on myself, but it feels real when I’m in it. It feels like I deserve to feel this bad because I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event in your past?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one big traumatic event I can point to, like something obvious that happened to me where I’d say, “Yes, that’s the thing.” Nothing like that is coming to mind right now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had hard things happen, and I’ve seen upsetting things at work sometimes, just because taking care of people can be a lot, but I don’t know if I would call it trauma. I guess I don’t really know where the line is. Some things stick with me more than I want them to, but I don’t have one specific event that I’ve been thinking of as traumatic.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. And then after that I don’t feel rested at all, even if technically I slept some.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in, because it’s not just “I had a bad night of sleep.” It’s like my sleep doesn’t work right anymore. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. Like, I’ve heard people describe that as watching themselves from above or feeling like they’re not in their own body, and I can’t really say that’s happened to me.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of distant or not fully present, especially when I’m worn down, but I still feel like I’m me, inside my own body. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that outside-my-body feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s like my mind just keeps going back over every little thing I’ve ever done wrong, or could’ve done wrong. Especially with the patients I’ve taken care of. I’ll think about something small, like if I didn’t check on somebody fast enough, or if I sounded irritated when I shouldn’t have, or if I missed something I should’ve noticed. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I do it with my family too. I think about ways I should’ve helped more, or protected people better, or been more patient, or done something different. I keep replaying things and feeling like I failed them. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. I don’t think so… not like it’s happening again, no.

I mean, I do think about things from the past a lot, probably too much, and sometimes I get stuck replaying things in my head, like what I should’ve done differently or whether I messed something up. I get off an overnight shift and I should be exhausted, and I am exhausted, but I’ll still lie there wide awake for so long. Then when I do sleep, it’s all broken up, and I wake up scared, and by the time I’m supposed to get up, I feel like I never rested at all. So… maybe they’re disturbing dreams, or maybe it’s just waking up scared, but either way it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge when you're awake, outside of the times when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t think of myself as anxious all day, if that makes sense. I get stressed like anybody, especially with work and trying to function when I’m tired, but I don’t know that I’m walking around feeling panicky or anything.\n\nIt’s more noticeable when I’m trying to sleep, or when I start thinking about whether I’m going to be able to sleep. During the day, or when I’m awake and doing things, I guess I can feel tense sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being worn down. I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. So… maybe a little on edge at times, but I wouldn’t know how to separate it from being exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving or re-experiencing a moment from your past as if it were happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people describe it, like suddenly being back there and seeing it all again. I don’t really have a moment like that that comes back. At least not that I can name.\n\nSometimes I get stuck thinking about things, like going over stuff in my head or feeling bad about things that happened, but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually there again. It’s more just thoughts, or feelings, or me trying to figure out why I feel the way I do. I don’t get, like, pictures taking over or anything where I lose track of where I am.\n\nI guess if you mean do I have memories that bother me, maybe sometimes, but not like reliving something. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right. I just don’t think that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things, places, or activities because they make you feel uncomfortable or remind you of something unpleasant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in a clear way. I mean, I avoid things sometimes, but I think it’s more because I’m tired or I don’t feel up to dealing with people, not because a place reminds me of something specific.\n\nLike, if I don’t go somewhere, it’s usually because it feels like too much effort, or I’m worried I won’t have the energy to get through it, or I just don’t want to be around noise and conversation. But I can’t think of a certain place or activity where I’m like, “I can’t go there because it brings something back.” It’s not like that.\n\nThere are things that make me uncomfortable, sure, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed myself avoiding a particular street, or a certain kind of room, or people because of some memory. If I’m avoiding anything, it’s more just… life, I guess. But not because it reminds me of something unpleasant, at least not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're in a safe environment?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not constantly. I mean, I notice things around me sometimes, like if someone is acting weird or if I’m walking somewhere and it feels uncomfortable, but I don’t think I’m always scanning for danger, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMost of the time when I’m in a safe place, I don’t feel like something bad is about to happen from outside, exactly. I might feel uneasy or tense sometimes, but I can’t say I’m watching doors or checking every sound or looking for threats all the time. It’s more like I’m in my head, or I’m just tired and trying to get through whatever I’m doing.\n\nI guess I do pay attention if I’m alone somewhere or if it’s late, but that feels kind of normal to me. I wouldn’t say I’m on guard all the time in places I know are safe. At least I haven’t noticed that about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy doing or places you used to go that you no longer do or visit because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s actually been one of the bigger things, I think. I used to have more of a routine outside of just work and going home, and now it feels like everything has kind of narrowed down to just trying to make it through the day.\n\nI used to go to church more, and that used to feel like something that kept me connected to people. I wasn’t always super involved every single week, but I went, and I knew people there, and it felt like a place I could show up. Lately I just… I don’t go. It feels like too much to get myself together and be around everyone, and then I feel bad because I know people probably notice, or maybe they don’t, I don’t know. But I’ve pulled back from that.\n\nAnd Zumba too. I used to like that, even if I wasn’t great at it. It was something fun, something where I could move around and not think so much. But I haven’t been going. I tell myself I’m too tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t. It just feels impossible to add anything extra when I’m already barely handling work.\n\nI was mentoring too, and I’ve backed away from that. That one makes me feel really guilty, because it’s not just me missing a class or something. It’s like I was supposed to be there for somebody, and now I can barely be there for myself. I just get home and I’m done. I don’t have anything left in me.\n\nEven work has gotten hard in a way that scares me, because I’m a personal care aide, so I have to be present and careful with people. And lately I can’t keep up with my care visits the way I should. I don’t feel like I can do them safely anymore sometimes. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I got so overwhelmed and stuck that I couldn’t finish it. I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. And then after that I don’t feel rested at all, even if technically I slept some.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in, because it’s not just “I had a bad night of sleep.” It’s like my sleep doesn’t work right anymore. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. Like, I’ve heard people describe that as watching themselves from above or feeling like they’re not in their own body, and I can’t really say that’s happened to me.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of distant or not fully present, especially when I’m worn down, but I still feel like I’m me, inside my own body. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that outside-my-body feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s like my mind just keeps going back over every little thing I’ve ever done wrong, or could’ve done wrong. Especially with the patients I’ve taken care of. I’ll think about something small, like if I didn’t check on somebody fast enough, or if I sounded irritated when I shouldn’t have, or if I missed something I should’ve noticed. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I do it with my family too. I think about ways I should’ve helped more, or protected people better, or been more patient, or done something different. I keep replaying things and feeling like I failed them. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. It feels like God is mad at me. Like He sees all the ways I didn’t protect people right, or didn’t do enough, and He’s angry with me for it. I know people might say I’m being too hard on myself, but it feels real when I’m in it. It feels like I deserve to feel this bad because I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event in your past?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one big traumatic event I can point to, like something obvious that happened to me where I’d say, “Yes, that’s the thing.” Nothing like that is coming to mind right now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had hard things happen, and I’ve seen upsetting things at work sometimes, just because taking care of people can be a lot, but I don’t know if I would call it trauma. I guess I don’t really know where the line is. Some things stick with me more than I want them to, but I don’t have one specific event that I’ve been thinking of as traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or images from your past that feel very vivid and real, like they're happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like it’s happening again, no.\n\nI mean, I do think about things from the past a lot, probably too much, and sometimes I get stuck replaying things in my head, like what I should’ve done differently or whether I messed something up. But it feels more like remembering or worrying, if that makes sense. It doesn’t feel like I’m back there or like I’m seeing it like a movie in front of me.\n\nI don’t really get vivid images where I lose track of where I am or anything like that. Sometimes a memory can feel emotionally strong, like it bothers me more than I think it should, but I still know I’m here and that it’s just me thinking about it. So… no, I don’t think I have memories that feel like they’re literally happening again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. It feels like God is mad at me. Like He sees all the ways I didn’t protect people right, or didn’t do enough, and He’s angry with me for it. I know people might say I’m being too hard on myself, but it feels real when I’m in it. It feels like I deserve to feel this bad because I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event in your past?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one big traumatic event I can point to, like something obvious that happened to me where I’d say, “Yes, that’s the thing.” Nothing like that is coming to mind right now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had hard things happen, and I’ve seen upsetting things at work sometimes, just because taking care of people can be a lot, but I don’t know if I would call it trauma. I guess I don’t really know where the line is. Some things stick with me more than I want them to, but I don’t have one specific event that I’ve been thinking of as traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or images from your past that feel very vivid and real, like they're happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like it’s happening again, no.\n\nI mean, I do think about things from the past a lot, probably too much, and sometimes I get stuck replaying things in my head, like what I should’ve done differently or whether I messed something up. But it feels more like remembering or worrying, if that makes sense. It doesn’t feel like I’m back there or like I’m seeing it like a movie in front of me.\n\nI don’t really get vivid images where I lose track of where I am or anything like that. Sometimes a memory can feel emotionally strong, like it bothers me more than I think it should, but I still know I’m here and that it’s just me thinking about it. So… no, I don’t think I have memories that feel like they’re literally happening again.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever have moments where you feel like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not necessarily traumatic?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… I mean, I can get really caught up thinking about things, like going over them again and again in my head, but I don’t know if I’d call it reliving.

Like, if I remember something I feel bad about, I might sit there and replay what I said or what I did, and I can get upset all over again. Sometimes it feels like my whole mood drops from just thinking about it. But I still know I’m here. I’m not suddenly back in that moment, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my brain won’t let it go, not like I’m actually there again.

So… no, I haven’t really noticed moments where it feels like I’m reliving something. More like I’m stuck remembering and picking it apart.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m just not sleeping right anymore. I work overnights, so I’m used to my sleep being kind of backwards, but this is different. I’ll get off a shift feeling like I should be exhausted, like my body is heavy and I need to crash, and then I lie down and I’m just wide awake. I’ll be staring at the ceiling, turning over and over, and it feels like my mind won’t shut off even though I’m so tired.\n\nAnd then when I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. It’s more just thoughts, or feelings, or me trying to figure out why I feel the way I do. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right. I just don’t think that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things, places, or activities because they make you feel uncomfortable or remind you of something unpleasant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in a clear way. But I can’t think of a certain place or activity where I’m like, “I can’t go there because it brings something back.” It’s not like that.\n\nThere are things that make me uncomfortable, sure, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed myself avoiding a particular street, or a certain kind of room, or people because of some memory. If I’m avoiding anything, it’s more just… life, I guess. But not because it reminds me of something unpleasant, at least not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're in a safe environment?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not constantly. I mean, I notice things around me sometimes, like if someone is acting weird or if I’m walking somewhere and it feels uncomfortable, but I don’t think I’m always scanning for danger, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMost of the time when I’m in a safe place, I don’t feel like something bad is about to happen from outside, exactly. I might feel uneasy or tense sometimes, but I can’t say I’m watching doors or checking every sound or looking for threats all the time. It’s more like I’m in my head, or I’m just tired and trying to get through whatever I’m doing.\n\nI guess I do pay attention if I’m alone somewhere or if it’s late, but that feels kind of normal to me. I wouldn’t say I’m on guard all the time in places I know are safe. At least I haven’t noticed that about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy doing or places you used to go that you no longer do or visit because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I wasn’t always super involved every single week, but I went, and I knew people there, and it felt like a place I could show up. Lately I just… I don’t go. I just get home and I’m done. I don’t have anything left in me.\n\nEven work has gotten hard in a way that scares me, because I’m a personal care aide, so I have to be present and careful with people. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I got so overwhelmed and stuck that I couldn’t finish it. I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. It’s more like I’ll finally drift off after lying there forever, and then I wake up scared, like my body jolts awake before my brain even catches up. Sometimes I’m not even sure what I was dreaming about, I just wake up with that awful feeling, like something bad was happening or about to happen.\n\nIt’s been happening enough that sleep doesn’t feel like sleep anymore. I get off an overnight shift and I should be exhausted, and I am exhausted, but I’ll still lie there wide awake for so long. Then when I do sleep, it’s all broken up, and I wake up scared, and by the time I’m supposed to get up, I feel like I never rested at all. So… maybe they’re disturbing dreams, or maybe it’s just waking up scared, but either way it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge when you're awake, outside of the times when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t think of myself as anxious all day, if that makes sense. I get stressed like anybody, especially with work and trying to function when I’m tired, but I don’t know that I’m walking around feeling panicky or anything.\n\nIt’s more noticeable when I’m trying to sleep, or when I start thinking about whether I’m going to be able to sleep. During the day, or when I’m awake and doing things, I guess I can feel tense sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being worn down. I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. So… maybe a little on edge at times, but I wouldn’t know how to separate it from being exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving or re-experiencing a moment from your past as if it were happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people describe it, like suddenly being back there and seeing it all again. I don’t really have a moment like that that comes back. At least not that I can name.\n\nSometimes I get stuck thinking about things, like going over stuff in my head or feeling bad about things that happened, but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually there again. It’s more just thoughts, or feelings, or me trying to figure out why I feel the way I do. I don’t get, like, pictures taking over or anything where I lose track of where I am.\n\nI guess if you mean do I have memories that bother me, maybe sometimes, but not like reliving something. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right. I just don’t think that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things, places, or activities because they make you feel uncomfortable or remind you of something unpleasant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in a clear way. I mean, I avoid things sometimes, but I think it’s more because I’m tired or I don’t feel up to dealing with people, not because a place reminds me of something specific.\n\nLike, if I don’t go somewhere, it’s usually because it feels like too much effort, or I’m worried I won’t have the energy to get through it, or I just don’t want to be around noise and conversation. But I can’t think of a certain place or activity where I’m like, “I can’t go there because it brings something back.” It’s not like that.\n\nThere are things that make me uncomfortable, sure, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed myself avoiding a particular street, or a certain kind of room, or people because of some memory. If I’m avoiding anything, it’s more just… life, I guess. But not because it reminds me of something unpleasant, at least not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're in a safe environment?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not constantly. I mean, I notice things around me sometimes, like if someone is acting weird or if I’m walking somewhere and it feels uncomfortable, but I don’t think I’m always scanning for danger, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMost of the time when I’m in a safe place, I don’t feel like something bad is about to happen from outside, exactly. I might feel uneasy or tense sometimes, but I can’t say I’m watching doors or checking every sound or looking for threats all the time. It’s more like I’m in my head, or I’m just tired and trying to get through whatever I’m doing.\n\nI guess I do pay attention if I’m alone somewhere or if it’s late, but that feels kind of normal to me. I wouldn’t say I’m on guard all the time in places I know are safe. At least I haven’t noticed that about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy doing or places you used to go that you no longer do or visit because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s actually been one of the bigger things, I think. I used to have more of a routine outside of just work and going home, and now it feels like everything has kind of narrowed down to just trying to make it through the day.\n\nI used to go to church more, and that used to feel like something that kept me connected to people. I wasn’t always super involved every single week, but I went, and I knew people there, and it felt like a place I could show up. Lately I just… I don’t go. It feels like too much to get myself together and be around everyone, and then I feel bad because I know people probably notice, or maybe they don’t, I don’t know. But I’ve pulled back from that.\n\nAnd Zumba too. I used to like that, even if I wasn’t great at it. It was something fun, something where I could move around and not think so much. But I haven’t been going. I tell myself I’m too tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t. It just feels impossible to add anything extra when I’m already barely handling work.\n\nI was mentoring too, and I’ve backed away from that. That one makes me feel really guilty, because it’s not just me missing a class or something. It’s like I was supposed to be there for somebody, and now I can barely be there for myself. I just get home and I’m done. I don’t have anything left in me.\n\nEven work has gotten hard in a way that scares me, because I’m a personal care aide, so I have to be present and careful with people. And lately I can’t keep up with my care visits the way I should. I don’t feel like I can do them safely anymore sometimes. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I got so overwhelmed and stuck that I couldn’t finish it. I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. And then after that I don’t feel rested at all, even if technically I slept some.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in, because it’s not just “I had a bad night of sleep.” It’s like my sleep doesn’t work right anymore. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I don’t know… I mean, I probably do sometimes, but I’m not sure what counts as trouble focusing.

I’ll get off a shift feeling like I should be exhausted, like my body is heavy and I need to crash, and then I lie down and I’m just wide awake. I’ll be staring at the ceiling, turning over and over, and it feels like my mind won’t shut off even though I’m so tired.\n\nAnd then when I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel like real sleep. I keep waking up scared, like startled awake, and I don’t even feel rested after. It’s like I’m sleeping in little broken pieces, and then I wake up feeling like I never slept at all. It’s been wearing me down, and I didn’t really know what else to do, so… I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced anything particularly stressful or traumatic recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not one big thing that I can point to and say, “That’s when it started,” if that’s what you mean. Nothing like an accident or somebody attacking me or anything like that.\n\nI’ve been trying to think about it because people keep asking, like, “Did something happen?” and I feel bad because I don’t have a good answer. My life has stress, sure, but it’s more just regular stuff piled up, I guess. Work can be a lot, especially doing overnights and taking care of people when you’re already tired, but I wouldn’t call it one specific traumatic thing recently. It’s more like… I don’t know, everything just started feeling heavier, and then the sleep got worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring nightmares or disturbing dreams?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares exactly, because I don’t always remember a whole dream like a movie or anything. It’s more like I’ll finally drift off after lying there forever, and then I wake up scared, like my body jolts awake before my brain even catches up. Sometimes I’m not even sure what I was dreaming about, I just wake up with that awful feeling, like something bad was happening or about to happen.\n\nIt’s been happening enough that sleep doesn’t feel like sleep anymore. I get off an overnight shift and I should be exhausted, and I am exhausted, but I’ll still lie there wide awake for so long. Then when I do sleep, it’s all broken up, and I wake up scared, and by the time I’m supposed to get up, I feel like I never rested at all. So… maybe they’re disturbing dreams, or maybe it’s just waking up scared, but either way it’s really wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge when you're awake, outside of the times when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I don’t think of myself as anxious all day, if that makes sense. I get stressed like anybody, especially with work and trying to function when I’m tired, but I don’t know that I’m walking around feeling panicky or anything.\n\nIt’s more noticeable when I’m trying to sleep, or when I start thinking about whether I’m going to be able to sleep. During the day, or when I’m awake and doing things, I guess I can feel tense sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s anxiety or just being worn down. I haven’t really paid attention to it that way. So… maybe a little on edge at times, but I wouldn’t know how to separate it from being exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself reliving or re-experiencing a moment from your past as if it were happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people describe it, like suddenly being back there and seeing it all again. I don’t really have a moment like that that comes back. At least not that I can name.\n\nSometimes I get stuck thinking about things, like going over stuff in my head or feeling bad about things that happened, but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually there again. It’s more just thoughts, or feelings, or me trying to figure out why I feel the way I do. I don’t get, like, pictures taking over or anything where I lose track of where I am.\n\nI guess if you mean do I have memories that bother me, maybe sometimes, but not like reliving something. I don’t know if I’m explaining that right. I just don’t think that happens to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things, places, or activities because they make you feel uncomfortable or remind you of something unpleasant?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in a clear way. I mean, I avoid things sometimes, but I think it’s more because I’m tired or I don’t feel up to dealing with people, not because a place reminds me of something specific.\n\nLike, if I don’t go somewhere, it’s usually because it feels like too much effort, or I’m worried I won’t have the energy to get through it, or I just don’t want to be around noise and conversation. But I can’t think of a certain place or activity where I’m like, “I can’t go there because it brings something back.” It’s not like that.\n\nThere are things that make me uncomfortable, sure, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I haven’t noticed myself avoiding a particular street, or a certain kind of room, or people because of some memory. If I’m avoiding anything, it’s more just… life, I guess. But not because it reminds me of something unpleasant, at least not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're in a safe environment?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not constantly. I mean, I notice things around me sometimes, like if someone is acting weird or if I’m walking somewhere and it feels uncomfortable, but I don’t think I’m always scanning for danger, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMost of the time when I’m in a safe place, I don’t feel like something bad is about to happen from outside, exactly. I might feel uneasy or tense sometimes, but I can’t say I’m watching doors or checking every sound or looking for threats all the time. It’s more like I’m in my head, or I’m just tired and trying to get through whatever I’m doing.\n\nI guess I do pay attention if I’m alone somewhere or if it’s late, but that feels kind of normal to me. I wouldn’t say I’m on guard all the time in places I know are safe. At least I haven’t noticed that about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy doing or places you used to go that you no longer do or visit because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s actually been one of the bigger things, I think. I used to have more of a routine outside of just work and going home, and now it feels like everything has kind of narrowed down to just trying to make it through the day.\n\nI used to go to church more, and that used to feel like something that kept me connected to people. I wasn’t always super involved every single week, but I went, and I knew people there, and it felt like a place I could show up. Lately I just… I don’t go. It feels like too much to get myself together and be around everyone, and then I feel bad because I know people probably notice, or maybe they don’t, I don’t know. But I’ve pulled back from that.\n\nAnd Zumba too. I used to like that, even if I wasn’t great at it. It was something fun, something where I could move around and not think so much. But I haven’t been going. I tell myself I’m too tired, or I’ll go next time, but then next time comes and I still don’t. It just feels impossible to add anything extra when I’m already barely handling work.\n\nI was mentoring too, and I’ve backed away from that. That one makes me feel really guilty, because it’s not just me missing a class or something. It’s like I was supposed to be there for somebody, and now I can barely be there for myself. I just get home and I’m done. I don’t have anything left in me.\n\nEven work has gotten hard in a way that scares me, because I’m a personal care aide, so I have to be present and careful with people. And lately I can’t keep up with my care visits the way I should. I don’t feel like I can do them safely anymore sometimes. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I got so overwhelmed and stuck that I couldn’t finish it. I was just standing there, or trying to push through, but I couldn’t get my thoughts together enough to do what I was supposed to do.\n\nSo… yeah, it’s not just that I stopped doing fun things. It feels like I’ve stopped everything except the bare minimum, and even the bare minimum is starting to feel like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or ashamed about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: Yes… all the time. It’s like my mind won’t leave it alone. I keep going back over every little thing I’ve done wrong, or think I did wrong, even small things that maybe other people wouldn’t even remember. With patients, especially, I’ll think, did I miss something, did I say the wrong thing, did I not protect them the way I should have, did I rush, did I fail them somehow. And then I’ll replay it over and over until it feels bigger and bigger.\n\nAnd with my family too. I think about all the ways I haven’t been there enough, or how I should have done more, or been stronger, or paid better attention. It doesn’t really stop at one mistake. It turns into this feeling like maybe I’m just… not good. Like I’m a worthless aide, and not really useful to anybody.\n\nAnd I feel ashamed saying that because I know people might tell me I’m being too hard on myself, but it doesn’t feel that way inside. It feels true when I’m thinking it. Like God is mad at me too, like I didn’t protect people better, like I was supposed to do more and I failed. I keep asking myself why I couldn’t just do things right. It’s exhausting, but I can’t seem to let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been keeping track, like I haven’t weighed myself regularly or anything. My clothes… I don’t know, they maybe feel about the same? I haven’t noticed anything really dramatic where I’d say, “Oh, I lost a lot,” or “I gained a lot.”\n\nI guess with how things have been, I’m not paying close attention to my body like that. I just kind of get dressed and try to get through the day. So if there has been a change, I don’t know that I could say for sure. Nothing obvious that I’ve noticed, though.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that you think might have triggered your sleep problems, even if it doesn't feel directly related to a traumatic event?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one specific thing that set it off. It’s not like I can point to one night and say, “That’s when it started,” or like it was because of one clear traumatic thing. It just… my sleep got broken up, and now it feels like I can’t get it back to normal.\n\nA lot of it happens after my overnight shifts. I’ll get off work exhausted, like my body should just shut down, but then I get home and I’m lying there wide awake. I’ll be tired, but my mind won’t settle, and I’ll just stare or turn over and over. Then if I finally do drift off, it doesn’t feel peaceful. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body is already on alert before I even know why. And then after that I don’t feel rested at all, even if technically I slept some.\n\nThat’s honestly part of why I came in, because it’s not just “I had a bad night of sleep.” It’s like my sleep doesn’t work right anymore. I’m either awake when I should be sleeping, or I’m waking up frightened, or I get up feeling like I never really rested in the first place. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. Like, I’ve heard people describe that as watching themselves from above or feeling like they’re not in their own body, and I can’t really say that’s happened to me.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of distant or not fully present, especially when I’m worn down, but I still feel like I’m me, inside my own body. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that outside-my-body feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s like my mind just keeps going back over every little thing I’ve ever done wrong, or could’ve done wrong. Especially with the patients I’ve taken care of. I’ll think about something small, like if I didn’t check on somebody fast enough, or if I sounded irritated when I shouldn’t have, or if I missed something I should’ve noticed. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I keep replaying things and feeling like I failed them. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. It feels like God is mad at me. Like He sees all the ways I didn’t protect people right, or didn’t do enough, and He’s angry with me for it. I know people might say I’m being too hard on myself, but it feels real when I’m in it. It feels like I deserve to feel this bad because I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event in your past?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one big traumatic event I can point to, like something obvious that happened to me where I’d say, “Yes, that’s the thing.” Nothing like that is coming to mind right now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had hard things happen, and I’ve seen upsetting things at work sometimes, just because taking care of people can be a lot, but I don’t know if I would call it trauma. I guess I don’t really know where the line is. Some things stick with me more than I want them to, but I don’t have one specific event that I’ve been thinking of as traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or images from your past that feel very vivid and real, like they're happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like it’s happening again, no.\n\nI mean, I do think about things from the past a lot, probably too much, and sometimes I get stuck replaying things in my head, like what I should’ve done differently or whether I messed something up. Sometimes it feels like my whole mood drops from just thinking about it. But I still know I’m here. I’m not suddenly back in that moment, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my brain won’t let it go, not like I’m actually there again.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed moments where it feels like I’m reliving something. But then other times I can get through things if I really have to, especially if someone’s depending on me or it’s something right in front of me. So I don’t know if it’s focus exactly, or if it’s just that my mind feels tired and kind of… slow.\n\nWhen I’m awake, it’s not like I’m always distracted by outside things. It’s more like I’ll try to pay attention and my thoughts sort of drift off, or I lose the thread of what I was doing. So I don’t know what triggered it exactly… I just know it’s been happening and it’s wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself from somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, I don’t think. Like, I’ve heard people describe that as watching themselves from above or feeling like they’re not in their own body, and I can’t really say that’s happened to me.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of distant or not fully present, especially when I’m worn down, but I still feel like I’m me, inside my own body. I’m not looking at myself from somewhere else. It’s more like I’m tired or foggy and trying to get through the moment, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that outside-my-body feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. A lot. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s like my mind just keeps going back over every little thing I’ve ever done wrong, or could’ve done wrong. Especially with the patients I’ve taken care of. I’ll think about something small, like if I didn’t check on somebody fast enough, or if I sounded irritated when I shouldn’t have, or if I missed something I should’ve noticed. And then it turns into this whole thing in my head where I’m not just someone who made a mistake, I’m a bad aide. Like I shouldn’t be trusted to take care of people.\n\nAnd it’s not only work. I do it with my family too. I think about ways I should’ve helped more, or protected people better, or been more patient, or done something different. I keep replaying things and feeling like I failed them. Like I’m just this burden or this disappointment, and people would be better off if I wasn’t causing problems or needing anything.\n\nAnd… I don’t know how to explain this without sounding strange, but sometimes it feels bigger than just me feeling guilty. It feels like God is mad at me. Like He sees all the ways I didn’t protect people right, or didn’t do enough, and He’s angry with me for it. I know people might say I’m being too hard on myself, but it feels real when I’m in it. It feels like I deserve to feel this bad because I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event in your past?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there’s one big traumatic event I can point to, like something obvious that happened to me where I’d say, “Yes, that’s the thing.” Nothing like that is coming to mind right now.\n\nI mean, I’ve had hard things happen, and I’ve seen upsetting things at work sometimes, just because taking care of people can be a lot, but I don’t know if I would call it trauma. I guess I don’t really know where the line is. Some things stick with me more than I want them to, but I don’t have one specific event that I’ve been thinking of as traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or images from your past that feel very vivid and real, like they're happening again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like it’s happening again, no.\n\nI mean, I do think about things from the past a lot, probably too much, and sometimes I get stuck replaying things in my head, like what I should’ve done differently or whether I messed something up. But it feels more like remembering or worrying, if that makes sense. It doesn’t feel like I’m back there or like I’m seeing it like a movie in front of me.\n\nI don’t really get vivid images where I lose track of where I am or anything like that. Sometimes a memory can feel emotionally strong, like it bothers me more than I think it should, but I still know I’m here and that it’s just me thinking about it. So… no, I don’t think I have memories that feel like they’re literally happening again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not necessarily traumatic?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I can get really caught up thinking about things, like going over them again and again in my head, but I don’t know if I’d call it reliving.\n\nLike, if I remember something I feel bad about, I might sit there and replay what I said or what I did, and I can get upset all over again. Sometimes it feels like my whole mood drops from just thinking about it. But I still know I’m here. I’m not suddenly back in that moment, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my brain won’t let it go, not like I’m actually there again.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed moments where it feels like I’m reliving something. Yeah… I guess I do, but it’s hard for me to say how much is “hard to concentrate” and how much is just me feeling worn out or slow.

Like if I’m trying to do something, I can be looking right at it and still not really taking it in. I’ll start a task and then kind of stop, not because I decided to, but because my mind just drifts or gets blank. Then I’m like, wait, what was I just doing? Even little things can feel like they take more effort than they should. Reading is like that too sometimes—I’ll read the same line a bunch of times and realize I have no idea what I just read.

But I don’t know… I can still sometimes push through if I have to. So I’m not sure if I’d say I can’t concentrate, exactly. It’s more like staying with something takes a lot more work than it used to, and I don’t always trust my mind to stay on track.
